# Sample documents

Generated by `scripts/sample_docs.py` from the files in `docs/`. Don't
edit by hand. The CommonMark spec is by John MacFarlane, under
CC-BY-SA 4.0 (see NOTICE at the top of the repo).

    export class SampleDocument(
      public name: String,
      public text: String,
      public gfm: Boolean,
    ) {}

Each text is built from short literals, so each backend compiles it
easily.

    let commonMarkSpec(): String {
      let out = new StringBuilder();
      out.append("---\u{A}title: CommonMark Spec\u{A}author: John MacFarlane\u{A}version: '0.31.2'\u{A}date: '2024-01-28'\u{A}license: '[CC-BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/)'\u{A}...\u{A}\u{A}# Introduction\u{A}\u{A}## What is Markdown?\u{A}\u{A}Markdown is a plain text format for writing structured documents,\u{A}based on conventions for indicating formatting in email\u{A}and usenet posts.  It was developed by John Gruber (with\u{A}help from Aaron Swartz) and released in 2004 in the form of a\u{A}[syntax description](https://daringfireball.net/projects/markdown/syntax)\u{A}and a Perl script (`Markdown.pl`) for converting Markdown to\u{A}HTML.  In the next decade, dozens of implementations were\u{A}developed in many languages.  Some extended the original\u{A}Markdown syntax with conventions for footnotes, tables, and\u{A}other document elements.  Some allowed Markdown documents to be\u{A}rendered in formats other than HTML.  Websites like Reddit,\u{A}StackOverflow, and GitHub had millions of people using Markdown.\u{A}And Markdown started to be used beyond the web, to author books,\u{A}articles, slide shows, letters, and lecture notes.\u{A}\u{A}What distinguishes Markdown from many other lightweight markup\u{A}syntaxes, which are often easier to write, is its readability.\u{A}As Gruber writes:\u{A}\u{A}> The overriding design goal for Markdown's formatting syntax is\u{A}> to make it as readable as possible. The idea is that a\u{A}> Markdown-formatted document should be publishable as-is, as\u{A}> plain text, without looking like it's been marked up with tags\u{A}> or formatting instructions.\u{A}> (<https://daringfireball.net/projects/markdown/>)\u{A}\u{A}The point can be illustrated by comparing a sample of\u{A}[AsciiDoc](https://asciidoc.org/) with\u{A}an equivalent sample of Markdown.  Here is a sample of\u{A}AsciiDoc from the AsciiDoc manual:\u{A}\u{A}```\u{A}1. List item one.\u{A}+\u{A}List item one continued with a second paragraph followed by an\u{A}Indented block.\u{A}+\u{A}.................\u{A}\u{24} ls *.sh\u{A}\u{24} mv *.sh ~/tmp\u{A}.................\u{A}+\u{A}List item continued with a third paragraph.\u{A}\u{A}2. List item two continued with an open block.\u{A}+\u{A}--\u{A}This parag");
      out.append("raph is part of the preceding list item.\u{A}\u{A}a. This list is nested and does not require explicit item\u{A}continuation.\u{A}+\u{A}This paragraph is part of the preceding list item.\u{A}\u{A}b. List item b.\u{A}\u{A}This paragraph belongs to item two of the outer list.\u{A}--\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}And here is the equivalent in Markdown:\u{A}```\u{A}1.  List item one.\u{A}\u{A}    List item one continued with a second paragraph followed by an\u{A}    Indented block.\u{A}\u{A}        \u{24} ls *.sh\u{A}        \u{24} mv *.sh ~/tmp\u{A}\u{A}    List item continued with a third paragraph.\u{A}\u{A}2.  List item two continued with an open block.\u{A}\u{A}    This paragraph is part of the preceding list item.\u{A}\u{A}    1. This list is nested and does not require explicit item continuation.\u{A}\u{A}       This paragraph is part of the preceding list item.\u{A}\u{A}    2. List item b.\u{A}\u{A}    This paragraph belongs to item two of the outer list.\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}The AsciiDoc version is, arguably, easier to write. You don't need\u{A}to worry about indentation.  But the Markdown version is much easier\u{A}to read.  The nesting of list items is apparent to the eye in the\u{A}source, not just in the processed document.\u{A}\u{A}## Why is a spec needed?\u{A}\u{A}John Gruber's [canonical description of Markdown's\u{A}syntax](https://daringfireball.net/projects/markdown/syntax)\u{A}does not specify the syntax unambiguously.  Here are some examples of\u{A}questions it does not answer:\u{A}\u{A}1.  How much indentation is needed for a sublist?  The spec says that\u{A}    continuation paragraphs need to be indented four spaces, but is\u{A}    not fully explicit about sublists.  It is natural to think that\u{A}    they, too, must be indented four spaces, but `Markdown.pl` does\u{A}    not require that.  This is hardly a \u{22}corner case,\u{22} and divergences\u{A}    between implementations on this issue often lead to surprises for\u{A}    users in real documents. (See [this comment by John\u{A}    Gruber](https://web.archive.org/web/20170611172104/http://article.gmane.org/gmane.text.markdown.general/1997).)\u{A}\u{A}2.  Is a blank line needed before a block quote or heading?\u{A}    Most implementations do not require the blank ");
      out.append("line.  However,\u{A}    this can lead to unexpected results in hard-wrapped text, and\u{A}    also to ambiguities in parsing (note that some implementations\u{A}    put the heading inside the blockquote, while others do not).\u{A}    (John Gruber has also spoken [in favor of requiring the blank\u{A}    lines](https://web.archive.org/web/20170611172104/http://article.gmane.org/gmane.text.markdown.general/2146).)\u{A}\u{A}3.  Is a blank line needed before an indented code block?\u{A}    (`Markdown.pl` requires it, but this is not mentioned in the\u{A}    documentation, and some implementations do not require it.)\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    paragraph\u{A}        code?\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}4.  What is the exact rule for determining when list items get\u{A}    wrapped in `<p>` tags?  Can a list be partially \u{22}loose\u{22} and partially\u{A}    \u{22}tight\u{22}?  What should we do with a list like this?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    1. one\u{A}\u{A}    2. two\u{A}    3. three\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}    Or this?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    1.  one\u{A}        - a\u{A}\u{A}        - b\u{A}    2.  two\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}    (There are some relevant comments by John Gruber\u{A}    [here](https://web.archive.org/web/20170611172104/http://article.gmane.org/gmane.text.markdown.general/2554).)\u{A}\u{A}5.  Can list markers be indented?  Can ordered list markers be right-aligned?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}     8. item 1\u{A}     9. item 2\u{A}    10. item 2a\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}6.  Is this one list with a thematic break in its second item,\u{A}    or two lists separated by a thematic break?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    * a\u{A}    * * * * *\u{A}    * b\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}7.  When list markers change from numbers to bullets, do we have\u{A}    two lists or one?  (The Markdown syntax description suggests two,\u{A}    but the perl scripts and many other implementations produce one.)\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    1. fee\u{A}    2. fie\u{A}    -  foe\u{A}    -  fum\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}8.  What are the precedence rules for the markers of inline structure?\u{A}    For example, is the following a valid link, or does the code span\u{A}    take precedence ?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    [a backtick (`)](/url) and [another backtick (`)](/url).\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}9. ");
      out.append(" What are the precedence rules for markers of emphasis and strong\u{A}    emphasis?  For example, how should the following be parsed?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    *foo *bar* baz*\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}10. What are the precedence rules between block-level and inline-level\u{A}    structure?  For example, how should the following be parsed?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    - `a long code span can contain a hyphen like this\u{A}      - and it can screw things up`\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}11. Can list items include section headings?  (`Markdown.pl` does not\u{A}    allow this, but does allow blockquotes to include headings.)\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    - # Heading\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}12. Can list items be empty?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    * a\u{A}    *\u{A}    * b\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}13. Can link references be defined inside block quotes or list items?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    > Blockquote [foo].\u{A}    >\u{A}    > [foo]: /url\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}14. If there are multiple definitions for the same reference, which takes\u{A}    precedence?\u{A}\u{A}    ``` markdown\u{A}    [foo]: /url1\u{A}    [foo]: /url2\u{A}\u{A}    [foo][]\u{A}    ```\u{A}\u{A}In the absence of a spec, early implementers consulted `Markdown.pl`\u{A}to resolve these ambiguities.  But `Markdown.pl` was quite buggy, and\u{A}gave manifestly bad results in many cases, so it was not a\u{A}satisfactory replacement for a spec.\u{A}\u{A}Because there is no unambiguous spec, implementations have diverged\u{A}considerably.  As a result, users are often surprised to find that\u{A}a document that renders one way on one system (say, a GitHub wiki)\u{A}renders differently on another (say, converting to docbook using\u{A}pandoc).  To make matters worse, because nothing in Markdown counts\u{A}as a \u{22}syntax error,\u{22} the divergence often isn't discovered right away.\u{A}\u{A}## About this document\u{A}\u{A}This document attempts to specify Markdown syntax unambiguously.\u{A}It contains many examples with side-by-side Markdown and\u{A}HTML.  These are intended to double as conformance tests.  An\u{A}accompanying script `spec_tests.py` can be used to run the tests\u{A}against any Markdown program:\u{A}\u{A}    python test/spec_tests.py --spec spec.txt --program P");
      out.append("ROGRAM\u{A}\u{A}Since this document describes how Markdown is to be parsed into\u{A}an abstract syntax tree, it would have made sense to use an abstract\u{A}representation of the syntax tree instead of HTML.  But HTML is capable\u{A}of representing the structural distinctions we need to make, and the\u{A}choice of HTML for the tests makes it possible to run the tests against\u{A}an implementation without writing an abstract syntax tree renderer.\u{A}\u{A}Note that not every feature of the HTML samples is mandated by\u{A}the spec.  For example, the spec says what counts as a link\u{A}destination, but it doesn't mandate that non-ASCII characters in\u{A}the URL be percent-encoded.  To use the automatic tests,\u{A}implementers will need to provide a renderer that conforms to\u{A}the expectations of the spec examples (percent-encoding\u{A}non-ASCII characters in URLs).  But a conforming implementation\u{A}can use a different renderer and may choose not to\u{A}percent-encode non-ASCII characters in URLs.\u{A}\u{A}This document is generated from a text file, `spec.txt`, written\u{A}in Markdown with a small extension for the side-by-side tests.\u{A}The script `tools/makespec.py` can be used to convert `spec.txt` into\u{A}HTML or CommonMark (which can then be converted into other formats).\u{A}\u{A}In the examples, the `\u{2192}` character is used to represent tabs.\u{A}\u{A}# Preliminaries\u{A}\u{A}## Characters and lines\u{A}\u{A}Any sequence of [characters] is a valid CommonMark\u{A}document.\u{A}\u{A}A [character](@) is a Unicode code point.  Although some\u{A}code points (for example, combining accents) do not correspond to\u{A}characters in an intuitive sense, all code points count as characters\u{A}for purposes of this spec.\u{A}\u{A}This spec does not specify an encoding; it thinks of lines as composed\u{A}of [characters] rather than bytes.  A conforming parser may be limited\u{A}to a certain encoding.\u{A}\u{A}A [line](@) is a sequence of zero or more [characters]\u{A}other than line feed (`U+000A`) or carriage return (`U+000D`),\u{A}followed by a [line ending] or by the end of file.\u{A}\u{A}A [line ending](@) is a line feed (`U+000A`), a carriage retu");
      out.append("rn\u{A}(`U+000D`) not followed by a line feed, or a carriage return and a\u{A}following line feed.\u{A}\u{A}A line containing no characters, or a line containing only spaces\u{A}(`U+0020`) or tabs (`U+0009`), is called a [blank line](@).\u{A}\u{A}The following definitions of character classes will be used in this spec:\u{A}\u{A}A [Unicode whitespace character](@) is a character in the Unicode `Zs` general\u{A}category, or a tab (`U+0009`), line feed (`U+000A`), form feed (`U+000C`), or\u{A}carriage return (`U+000D`).\u{A}\u{A}[Unicode whitespace](@) is a sequence of one or more\u{A}[Unicode whitespace characters].\u{A}\u{A}A [tab](@) is `U+0009`.\u{A}\u{A}A [space](@) is `U+0020`.\u{A}\u{A}An [ASCII control character](@) is a character between `U+0000\u{2013}1F` (both\u{A}including) or `U+007F`.\u{A}\u{A}An [ASCII punctuation character](@)\u{A}is `!`, `\u{22}`, `#`, `\u{24}`, `%`, `&`, `'`, `(`, `)`,\u{A}`*`, `+`, `,`, `-`, `.`, `/` (U+0021\u{2013}2F), \u{A}`:`, `;`, `<`, `=`, `>`, `?`, `@` (U+003A\u{2013}0040),\u{A}`[`, `\u{5C}`, `]`, `^`, `_`, `` ` `` (U+005B\u{2013}0060), \u{A}`{`, `|`, `}`, or `~` (U+007B\u{2013}007E).\u{A}\u{A}A [Unicode punctuation character](@) is a character in the Unicode `P`\u{A}(puncuation) or `S` (symbol) general categories.\u{A}\u{A}## Tabs\u{A}\u{A}Tabs in lines are not expanded to [spaces].  However,\u{A}in contexts where spaces help to define block structure,\u{A}tabs behave as if they were replaced by spaces with a tab stop\u{A}of 4 characters.\u{A}\u{A}Thus, for example, a tab can be used instead of four spaces\u{A}in an indented code block.  (Note, however, that internal\u{A}tabs are passed through as literal tabs, not expanded to\u{A}spaces.)\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{2192}foo\u{2192}baz\u{2192}\u{2192}bim\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{2192}baz\u{2192}\u{2192}bim\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}  \u{2192}foo\u{2192}baz\u{2192}\u{2192}bim\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{2192}baz\u{2192}\u{2192}bim\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    a\u{2192}a\u{A}    \u{1F50}\u{2192}a\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>a\u{2192}a\u{A}\u{1F50}\u{2192}a\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}In the following example, a continuation paragraph of a list\u{A}item is indented with a tab; this has exactly the same effect\u{A}as i");
      out.append("ndentation with four spaces would:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}  - foo\u{A}\u{A}\u{2192}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- foo\u{A}\u{A}\u{2192}\u{2192}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<pre><code>  bar\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}Normally the `>` that begins a block quote may be followed\u{A}optionally by a space, which is not considered part of the\u{A}content.  In the following case `>` is followed by a tab,\u{A}which is treated as if it were expanded into three spaces.\u{A}Since one of these spaces is considered part of the\u{A}delimiter, `foo` is considered to be indented six spaces\u{A}inside the block quote context, so we get an indented\u{A}code block starting with two spaces.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}>\u{2192}\u{2192}foo\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<pre><code>  foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}</blockquote>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}-\u{2192}\u{2192}foo\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<pre><code>  foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    foo\u{A}\u{2192}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}bar\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} - foo\u{A}   - bar\u{A}\u{2192} - baz\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>foo\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>bar\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>baz</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}#\u{2192}Foo\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>Foo</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}*\u{2192}*\u{2192}*\u{2192}\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Insecure characters\u{A}\u{A}For security reasons, the Unicode character `U+0000` must be replaced\u{A}with the REPLACEMENT CHARACTER (`U+FFFD`).\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Backslash escapes\u{A}\u{A}Any ASCII punctuation character may be backslash-escaped:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}!\u{5C}\u{22}\u{5C}#\u{5C}\u{24}\u{5C}%\u{5C}&\u{5C}'\u{5C}(\u{5C})\u{5C}*\u{5C}+\u{5C},\u{5C}-\u{5C}.\u{5C}/\u{5C}:\u{5C};\u{5C}<\u{5C}=\u{5C}>\u{5C}?\u{5C}@\u{5C}[\u{5C}\u{5C}\u{5C}]\u{5C}^\u{5C}_\u{5C}`\u{5C}{\u{5C}|\u{5C}}\u{5C}~\u{A}.\u{A}<p>!&quot;#\u{24}%&amp;'()*+,-./:;&lt;=&gt;?@[\u{5C}]^_`{|}~</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Backslashes before other characters are treated as lite");
      out.append("ral\u{A}backslashes:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}\u{2192}\u{5C}A\u{5C}a\u{5C} \u{5C}3\u{5C}\u{3C6}\u{5C}\u{AB}\u{A}.\u{A}<p>\u{5C}\u{2192}\u{5C}A\u{5C}a\u{5C} \u{5C}3\u{5C}\u{3C6}\u{5C}\u{AB}</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Escaped characters are treated as regular characters and do\u{A}not have their usual Markdown meanings:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}*not emphasized*\u{A}\u{5C}<br/> not a tag\u{A}\u{5C}[not a link](/foo)\u{A}\u{5C}`not code`\u{A}1\u{5C}. not a list\u{A}\u{5C}* not a list\u{A}\u{5C}# not a heading\u{A}\u{5C}[foo]: /url \u{22}not a reference\u{22}\u{A}\u{5C}&ouml; not a character entity\u{A}.\u{A}<p>*not emphasized*\u{A}&lt;br/&gt; not a tag\u{A}[not a link](/foo)\u{A}`not code`\u{A}1. not a list\u{A}* not a list\u{A}# not a heading\u{A}[foo]: /url &quot;not a reference&quot;\u{A}&amp;ouml; not a character entity</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If a backslash is itself escaped, the following character is not:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}\u{5C}*emphasis*\u{A}.\u{A}<p>\u{5C}<em>emphasis</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A backslash at the end of the line is a [hard line break]:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}foo\u{5C}\u{A}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<p>foo<br />\u{A}bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Backslash escapes do not work in code blocks, code spans, autolinks, or\u{A}raw HTML:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}`` \u{5C}[\u{5C}` ``\u{A}.\u{A}<p><code>\u{5C}[\u{5C}`</code></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    \u{5C}[\u{5C}]\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>\u{5C}[\u{5C}]\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~\u{A}\u{5C}[\u{5C}]\u{A}~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>\u{5C}[\u{5C}]\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<https://example.com?find=\u{5C}*>\u{A}.\u{A}<p><a href=\u{22}https://example.com?find=%5C*\u{22}>https://example.com?find=\u{5C}*</a></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<a href=\u{22}/bar\u{5C}/)\u{22}>\u{A}.\u{A}<a href=\u{22}/bar\u{5C}/)\u{22}>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}But they work in all other contexts, including URLs and link titles,\u{A}link references, and [info strings] in [fenced code blocks]:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}[foo](/bar\u{5C}* \u{22}ti\u{5C}*tle\u{22})\u{A}.\u{A}<p><a href=\u{22}/bar*\u{22} title=\u{22}ti*tle");
      out.append("\u{22}>foo</a></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}[foo]\u{A}\u{A}[foo]: /bar\u{5C}* \u{22}ti\u{5C}*tle\u{22}\u{A}.\u{A}<p><a href=\u{22}/bar*\u{22} title=\u{22}ti*tle\u{22}>foo</a></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}``` foo\u{5C}+bar\u{A}foo\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-foo+bar\u{22}>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Entity and numeric character references\u{A}\u{A}Valid HTML entity references and numeric character references\u{A}can be used in place of the corresponding Unicode character,\u{A}with the following exceptions:\u{A}\u{A}- Entity and character references are not recognized in code\u{A}  blocks and code spans.\u{A}\u{A}- Entity and character references cannot stand in place of\u{A}  special characters that define structural elements in\u{A}  CommonMark.  For example, although `&#42;` can be used\u{A}  in place of a literal `*` character, `&#42;` cannot replace\u{A}  `*` in emphasis delimiters, bullet list markers, or thematic\u{A}  breaks.\u{A}\u{A}Conforming CommonMark parsers need not store information about\u{A}whether a particular character was represented in the source\u{A}using a Unicode character or an entity reference.\u{A}\u{A}[Entity references](@) consist of `&` + any of the valid\u{A}HTML5 entity names + `;`. The\u{A}document <https://html.spec.whatwg.org/entities.json>\u{A}is used as an authoritative source for the valid entity\u{A}references and their corresponding code points.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&nbsp; &amp; &copy; &AElig; &Dcaron;\u{A}&frac34; &HilbertSpace; &DifferentialD;\u{A}&ClockwiseContourIntegral; &ngE;\u{A}.\u{A}<p>\u{A0} &amp; \u{A9} \u{C6} \u{10E}\u{A}\u{BE} \u{210B} \u{2146}\u{A}\u{2232} \u{2267}\u{338}</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}[Decimal numeric character\u{A}references](@)\u{A}consist of `&#` + a string of 1--7 arabic digits + `;`. A\u{A}numeric character reference is parsed as the corresponding\u{A}Unicode character. Invalid Unicode code points will be replaced by\u{A}the REPLACEMENT CHARACTER (`U+FFFD`).  For security reasons,\u{A}the code point `U+0000` will also be replaced by `U+FFFD`.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&#35; &#1234; &#992;");
      out.append(" &#0;\u{A}.\u{A}<p># \u{4D2} \u{3E0} \u{FFFD}</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}[Hexadecimal numeric character\u{A}references](@) consist of `&#` +\u{A}either `X` or `x` + a string of 1-6 hexadecimal digits + `;`.\u{A}They too are parsed as the corresponding Unicode character (this\u{A}time specified with a hexadecimal numeral instead of decimal).\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&#X22; &#XD06; &#xcab;\u{A}.\u{A}<p>&quot; \u{D06} \u{CAB}</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Here are some nonentities:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&nbsp &x; &#; &#x;\u{A}&#87654321;\u{A}&#abcdef0;\u{A}&ThisIsNotDefined; &hi?;\u{A}.\u{A}<p>&amp;nbsp &amp;x; &amp;#; &amp;#x;\u{A}&amp;#87654321;\u{A}&amp;#abcdef0;\u{A}&amp;ThisIsNotDefined; &amp;hi?;</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Although HTML5 does accept some entity references\u{A}without a trailing semicolon (such as `&copy`), these are not\u{A}recognized here, because it makes the grammar too ambiguous:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&copy\u{A}.\u{A}<p>&amp;copy</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Strings that are not on the list of HTML5 named entities are not\u{A}recognized as entity references either:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&MadeUpEntity;\u{A}.\u{A}<p>&amp;MadeUpEntity;</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Entity and numeric character references are recognized in any\u{A}context besides code spans or code blocks, including\u{A}URLs, [link titles], and [fenced code block][] [info strings]:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<a href=\u{22}&ouml;&ouml;.html\u{22}>\u{A}.\u{A}<a href=\u{22}&ouml;&ouml;.html\u{22}>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}[foo](/f&ouml;&ouml; \u{22}f&ouml;&ouml;\u{22})\u{A}.\u{A}<p><a href=\u{22}/f%C3%B6%C3%B6\u{22} title=\u{22}f\u{F6}\u{F6}\u{22}>foo</a></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}[foo]\u{A}\u{A}[foo]: /f&ouml;&ouml; \u{22}f&ouml;&ouml;\u{22}\u{A}.\u{A}<p><a href=\u{22}/f%C3%B6%C3%B6\u{22} title=\u{22}f\u{F6}\u{F6}\u{22}>foo</a></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}``` f&ouml;&ouml;\u{A}foo\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-f\u{F6}\u{F6}\u{22}>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````");
      out.append("````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Entity and numeric character references are treated as literal\u{A}text in code spans and code blocks:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}`f&ouml;&ouml;`\u{A}.\u{A}<p><code>f&amp;ouml;&amp;ouml;</code></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    f&ouml;f&ouml;\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>f&amp;ouml;f&amp;ouml;\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Entity and numeric character references cannot be used\u{A}in place of symbols indicating structure in CommonMark\u{A}documents.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&#42;foo&#42;\u{A}*foo*\u{A}.\u{A}<p>*foo*\u{A}<em>foo</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&#42; foo\u{A}\u{A}* foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p>* foo</p>\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}foo&#10;&#10;bar\u{A}.\u{A}<p>foo\u{A}\u{A}bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}&#9;foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p>\u{2192}foo</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}[a](url &quot;tit&quot;)\u{A}.\u{A}<p>[a](url &quot;tit&quot;)</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}# Blocks and inlines\u{A}\u{A}We can think of a document as a sequence of\u{A}[blocks](@)---structural elements like paragraphs, block\u{A}quotations, lists, headings, rules, and code blocks.  Some blocks (like\u{A}block quotes and list items) contain other blocks; others (like\u{A}headings and paragraphs) contain [inline](@) content---text,\u{A}links, emphasized text, images, code spans, and so on.\u{A}\u{A}## Precedence\u{A}\u{A}Indicators of block structure always take precedence over indicators\u{A}of inline structure.  So, for example, the following is a list with\u{A}two items, not a list with one item containing a code span:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- `one\u{A}- two`\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>`one</li>\u{A}<li>two`</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}This means that parsing can proceed in two steps:  first, the block\u{A}structure of the document can be discerned; second, text lines inside\u{A}paragraphs, headin");
      out.append("gs, and other block constructs can be parsed for inline\u{A}structure.  The second step requires information about link reference\u{A}definitions that will be available only at the end of the first\u{A}step.  Note that the first step requires processing lines in sequence,\u{A}but the second can be parallelized, since the inline parsing of\u{A}one block element does not affect the inline parsing of any other.\u{A}\u{A}## Container blocks and leaf blocks\u{A}\u{A}We can divide blocks into two types:\u{A}[container blocks](#container-blocks),\u{A}which can contain other blocks, and [leaf blocks](#leaf-blocks),\u{A}which cannot.\u{A}\u{A}# Leaf blocks\u{A}\u{A}This section describes the different kinds of leaf block that make up a\u{A}Markdown document.\u{A}\u{A}## Thematic breaks\u{A}\u{A}A line consisting of optionally up to three spaces of indentation, followed by a\u{A}sequence of three or more matching `-`, `_`, or `*` characters, each followed\u{A}optionally by any number of spaces or tabs, forms a\u{A}[thematic break](@).\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}***\u{A}---\u{A}___\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Wrong characters:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}+++\u{A}.\u{A}<p>+++</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}===\u{A}.\u{A}<p>===</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Not enough characters:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}--\u{A}**\u{A}__\u{A}.\u{A}<p>--\u{A}**\u{A}__</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Up to three spaces of indentation are allowed:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} ***\u{A}  ***\u{A}   ***\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Four spaces of indentation is too many:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    ***\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>***\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}    ***\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}***</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}More than three characters may be used:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}_____________________________________\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Spaces and tabs are a");
      out.append("llowed between the characters:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} - - -\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} **  * ** * ** * **\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}-     -      -      -\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Spaces and tabs are allowed at the end:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- - - -    \u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}However, no other characters may occur in the line:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}_ _ _ _ a\u{A}\u{A}a------\u{A}\u{A}---a---\u{A}.\u{A}<p>_ _ _ _ a</p>\u{A}<p>a------</p>\u{A}<p>---a---</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}It is required that all of the characters other than spaces or tabs be the same.\u{A}So, this is not a thematic break:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} *-*\u{A}.\u{A}<p><em>-</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Thematic breaks do not need blank lines before or after:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- foo\u{A}***\u{A}- bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>bar</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Thematic breaks can interrupt a paragraph:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}***\u{A}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo</p>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If a line of dashes that meets the above conditions for being a\u{A}thematic break could also be interpreted as the underline of a [setext\u{A}heading], the interpretation as a\u{A}[setext heading] takes precedence. Thus, for example,\u{A}this is a setext heading, not a paragraph followed by a thematic break:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}---\u{A}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}When both a thematic break and a list item are possible\u{A}interpretations of a line, the thematic break takes precedence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}* Foo\u{A}* * *\u{A}* Bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>Foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>Bar</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If you want a thematic break in a list item, use a ");
      out.append("different bullet:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- Foo\u{A}- * * *\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>Foo</li>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## ATX headings\u{A}\u{A}An [ATX heading](@)\u{A}consists of a string of characters, parsed as inline content, between an\u{A}opening sequence of 1--6 unescaped `#` characters and an optional\u{A}closing sequence of any number of unescaped `#` characters.\u{A}The opening sequence of `#` characters must be followed by spaces or tabs, or\u{A}by the end of line. The optional closing sequence of `#`s must be preceded by\u{A}spaces or tabs and may be followed by spaces or tabs only.  The opening\u{A}`#` character may be preceded by up to three spaces of indentation.  The raw\u{A}contents of the heading are stripped of leading and trailing space or tabs\u{A}before being parsed as inline content.  The heading level is equal to the number\u{A}of `#` characters in the opening sequence.\u{A}\u{A}Simple headings:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}# foo\u{A}## foo\u{A}### foo\u{A}#### foo\u{A}##### foo\u{A}###### foo\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>foo</h1>\u{A}<h2>foo</h2>\u{A}<h3>foo</h3>\u{A}<h4>foo</h4>\u{A}<h5>foo</h5>\u{A}<h6>foo</h6>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}More than six `#` characters is not a heading:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}####### foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p>####### foo</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}At least one space or tab is required between the `#` characters and the\u{A}heading's contents, unless the heading is empty.  Note that many\u{A}implementations currently do not require the space.  However, the\u{A}space was required by the\u{A}[original ATX implementation](http://www.aaronsw.com/2002/atx/atx.py),\u{A}and it helps prevent things like the following from being parsed as\u{A}headings:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}#5 bolt\u{A}\u{A}#hashtag\u{A}.\u{A}<p>#5 bolt</p>\u{A}<p>#hashtag</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}This is not a heading, because the first `#` is escaped:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}## foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p>## foo</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Contents are parsed as inlines:\u{A}\u{A}``````````````````````````");
      out.append("`````` example\u{A}# foo *bar* \u{5C}*baz\u{5C}*\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>foo <em>bar</em> *baz*</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Leading and trailing spaces or tabs are ignored in parsing inline content:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}#                  foo                     \u{A}.\u{A}<h1>foo</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Up to three spaces of indentation are allowed:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} ### foo\u{A}  ## foo\u{A}   # foo\u{A}.\u{A}<h3>foo</h3>\u{A}<h2>foo</h2>\u{A}<h1>foo</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Four spaces of indentation is too many:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    # foo\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code># foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}foo\u{A}    # bar\u{A}.\u{A}<p>foo\u{A}# bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A closing sequence of `#` characters is optional:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}## foo ##\u{A}  ###   bar    ###\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>foo</h2>\u{A}<h3>bar</h3>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}It need not be the same length as the opening sequence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}# foo ##################################\u{A}##### foo ##\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>foo</h1>\u{A}<h5>foo</h5>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Spaces or tabs are allowed after the closing sequence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}### foo ###     \u{A}.\u{A}<h3>foo</h3>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A sequence of `#` characters with anything but spaces or tabs following it\u{A}is not a closing sequence, but counts as part of the contents of the\u{A}heading:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}### foo ### b\u{A}.\u{A}<h3>foo ### b</h3>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The closing sequence must be preceded by a space or tab:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}# foo#\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>foo#</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Backslash-escaped `#` characters do not count as part\u{A}of the closing sequence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}### foo \u{5C}###\u{A}## foo #\u{5C}##\u{A}# foo \u{5C}#\u{A}.\u{A}<h3>foo ###</h3>\u{A}<h2>foo ###</h2>\u{A}<h1>foo #</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}");
      out.append("\u{A}\u{A}ATX headings need not be separated from surrounding content by blank\u{A}lines, and they can interrupt paragraphs:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}****\u{A}## foo\u{A}****\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<h2>foo</h2>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo bar\u{A}# baz\u{A}Bar foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo bar</p>\u{A}<h1>baz</h1>\u{A}<p>Bar foo</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}ATX headings can be empty:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}## \u{A}#\u{A}### ###\u{A}.\u{A}<h2></h2>\u{A}<h1></h1>\u{A}<h3></h3>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Setext headings\u{A}\u{A}A [setext heading](@) consists of one or more\u{A}lines of text, not interrupted by a blank line, of which the first line does not\u{A}have more than 3 spaces of indentation, followed by\u{A}a [setext heading underline].  The lines of text must be such\u{A}that, were they not followed by the setext heading underline,\u{A}they would be interpreted as a paragraph:  they cannot be\u{A}interpretable as a [code fence], [ATX heading][ATX headings],\u{A}[block quote][block quotes], [thematic break][thematic breaks],\u{A}[list item][list items], or [HTML block][HTML blocks].\u{A}\u{A}A [setext heading underline](@) is a sequence of\u{A}`=` characters or a sequence of `-` characters, with no more than 3\u{A}spaces of indentation and any number of trailing spaces or tabs.\u{A}\u{A}The heading is a level 1 heading if `=` characters are used in\u{A}the [setext heading underline], and a level 2 heading if `-`\u{A}characters are used.  The contents of the heading are the result\u{A}of parsing the preceding lines of text as CommonMark inline\u{A}content.\u{A}\u{A}In general, a setext heading need not be preceded or followed by a\u{A}blank line.  However, it cannot interrupt a paragraph, so when a\u{A}setext heading comes after a paragraph, a blank line is needed between\u{A}them.\u{A}\u{A}Simple examples:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo *bar*\u{A}=========\u{A}\u{A}Foo *bar*\u{A}---------\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>Foo <em>bar</em></h1>\u{A}<h2>Foo <em>bar</em></h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The content of the header may span more than one line:\u{A}\u{A}```````````");
      out.append("````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo *bar\u{A}baz*\u{A}====\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>Foo <em>bar\u{A}baz</em></h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}The contents are the result of parsing the headings's raw\u{A}content as inlines.  The heading's raw content is formed by\u{A}concatenating the lines and removing initial and final\u{A}spaces or tabs.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}  Foo *bar\u{A}baz*\u{2192}\u{A}====\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>Foo <em>bar\u{A}baz</em></h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The underlining can be any length:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}-------------------------\u{A}\u{A}Foo\u{A}=\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}<h1>Foo</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The heading content can be preceded by up to three spaces of indentation, and\u{A}need not line up with the underlining:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}   Foo\u{A}---\u{A}\u{A}  Foo\u{A}-----\u{A}\u{A}  Foo\u{A}  ===\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}<h1>Foo</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Four spaces of indentation is too many:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    Foo\u{A}    ---\u{A}\u{A}    Foo\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>Foo\u{A}---\u{A}\u{A}Foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The setext heading underline can be preceded by up to three spaces of\u{A}indentation, and may have trailing spaces or tabs:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}   ----      \u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Four spaces of indentation is too many:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}    ---\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}---</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The setext heading underline cannot contain internal spaces or tabs:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}= =\u{A}\u{A}Foo\u{A}--- -\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}= =</p>\u{A}<p>Foo</p>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Trailing spaces or tabs in the content line do not cause a hard line break:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo  \u{A}-----\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Nor does a backslash at the end:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{5C}\u{A}----\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo\u{5C}</h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Since indicators of block stru");
      out.append("cture take precedence over\u{A}indicators of inline structure, the following are setext headings:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}`Foo\u{A}----\u{A}`\u{A}\u{A}<a title=\u{22}a lot\u{A}---\u{A}of dashes\u{22}/>\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>`Foo</h2>\u{A}<p>`</p>\u{A}<h2>&lt;a title=&quot;a lot</h2>\u{A}<p>of dashes&quot;/&gt;</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The setext heading underline cannot be a [lazy continuation\u{A}line] in a list item or block quote:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}> Foo\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<p>Foo</p>\u{A}</blockquote>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}> foo\u{A}bar\u{A}===\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<p>foo\u{A}bar\u{A}===</p>\u{A}</blockquote>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- Foo\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>Foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A blank line is needed between a paragraph and a following\u{A}setext heading, since otherwise the paragraph becomes part\u{A}of the heading's content:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}Bar\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>Foo\u{A}Bar</h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}But in general a blank line is not required before or after\u{A}setext headings:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}---\u{A}Foo\u{A}---\u{A}Bar\u{A}---\u{A}Baz\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<h2>Foo</h2>\u{A}<h2>Bar</h2>\u{A}<p>Baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Setext headings cannot be empty:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{A}====\u{A}.\u{A}<p>====</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Setext heading text lines must not be interpretable as block\u{A}constructs other than paragraphs.  So, the line of dashes\u{A}in these examples gets interpreted as a thematic break:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}---\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- foo\u{A}-----\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    foo\u{A}---\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}> foo\u{A}-----\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<");
      out.append("/blockquote>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If you want a heading with `> foo` as its literal text, you can\u{A}use backslash escapes:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{5C}> foo\u{A}------\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>&gt; foo</h2>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}**Compatibility note:**  Most existing Markdown implementations\u{A}do not allow the text of setext headings to span multiple lines.\u{A}But there is no consensus about how to interpret\u{A}\u{A}``` markdown\u{A}Foo\u{A}bar\u{A}---\u{A}baz\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}One can find four different interpretations:\u{A}\u{A}1. paragraph \u{22}Foo\u{22}, heading \u{22}bar\u{22}, paragraph \u{22}baz\u{22}\u{A}2. paragraph \u{22}Foo bar\u{22}, thematic break, paragraph \u{22}baz\u{22}\u{A}3. paragraph \u{22}Foo bar --- baz\u{22}\u{A}4. heading \u{22}Foo bar\u{22}, paragraph \u{22}baz\u{22}\u{A}\u{A}We find interpretation 4 most natural, and interpretation 4\u{A}increases the expressive power of CommonMark, by allowing\u{A}multiline headings.  Authors who want interpretation 1 can\u{A}put a blank line after the first paragraph:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}\u{A}bar\u{A}---\u{A}baz\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo</p>\u{A}<h2>bar</h2>\u{A}<p>baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Authors who want interpretation 2 can put blank lines around\u{A}the thematic break,\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}bar\u{A}\u{A}---\u{A}\u{A}baz\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}bar</p>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<p>baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}or use a thematic break that cannot count as a [setext heading\u{A}underline], such as\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}bar\u{A}* * *\u{A}baz\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}bar</p>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}<p>baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Authors who want interpretation 3 can use backslash escapes:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}bar\u{A}\u{5C}---\u{A}baz\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}bar\u{A}---\u{A}baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Indented code blocks\u{A}\u{A}An [indented code block](@) is composed of one or more\u{A}[indented chunks] separated by blank lines.\u{A}An [indented chunk](@) is a sequence of non-blank lines,\u{A}each preceded by four or more spaces of indentation. The contents of the code\u{A}block are the literal contents of the lines, including trailing\u{A}[line endings], minus four space");
      out.append("s of indentation.\u{A}An indented code block has no [info string].\u{A}\u{A}An indented code block cannot interrupt a paragraph, so there must be\u{A}a blank line between a paragraph and a following indented code block.\u{A}(A blank line is not needed, however, between a code block and a following\u{A}paragraph.)\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    a simple\u{A}      indented code block\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>a simple\u{A}  indented code block\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If there is any ambiguity between an interpretation of indentation\u{A}as a code block and as indicating that material belongs to a [list\u{A}item][list items], the list item interpretation takes precedence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}  - foo\u{A}\u{A}    bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}1.  foo\u{A}\u{A}    - bar\u{A}.\u{A}<ol>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>bar</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}</li>\u{A}</ol>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The contents of a code block are literal text, and do not get parsed\u{A}as Markdown:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    <a/>\u{A}    *hi*\u{A}\u{A}    - one\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>&lt;a/&gt;\u{A}*hi*\u{A}\u{A}- one\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Here we have three chunks separated by blank lines:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    chunk1\u{A}\u{A}    chunk2\u{A}  \u{A} \u{A} \u{A}    chunk3\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>chunk1\u{A}\u{A}chunk2\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}chunk3\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Any initial spaces or tabs beyond four spaces of indentation will be included in\u{A}the content, even in interior blank lines:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    chunk1\u{A}      \u{A}      chunk2\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>chunk1\u{A}  \u{A}  chunk2\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}An indented code block cannot interrupt a paragraph.  (This\u{A}allows hanging indents and the like.)\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}Foo\u{A}    bar\u{A}\u{A}.\u{A}<p>Foo\u{A}bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}However, any non-blank line with fewer than four spaces of indentation ends\u{A}the code block immedi");
      out.append("ately.  So a paragraph may occur immediately\u{A}after indented code:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    foo\u{A}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}And indented code can occur immediately before and after other kinds of\u{A}blocks:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}# Heading\u{A}    foo\u{A}Heading\u{A}------\u{A}    foo\u{A}----\u{A}.\u{A}<h1>Heading</h1>\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<h2>Heading</h2>\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<hr />\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The first line can be preceded by more than four spaces of indentation:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}        foo\u{A}    bar\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>    foo\u{A}bar\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Blank lines preceding or following an indented code block\u{A}are not included in it:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}\u{A}    \u{A}    foo\u{A}    \u{A}\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Trailing spaces or tabs are included in the code block's content:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    foo  \u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>foo  \u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## Fenced code blocks\u{A}\u{A}A [code fence](@) is a sequence\u{A}of at least three consecutive backtick characters (`` ` ``) or\u{A}tildes (`~`).  (Tildes and backticks cannot be mixed.)\u{A}A [fenced code block](@)\u{A}begins with a code fence, preceded by up to three spaces of indentation.\u{A}\u{A}The line with the opening code fence may optionally contain some text\u{A}following the code fence; this is trimmed of leading and trailing\u{A}spaces or tabs and called the [info string](@). If the [info string] comes\u{A}after a backtick fence, it may not contain any backtick\u{A}characters.  (The reason for this restriction is that otherwise\u{A}some inline code would be incorrectly interpreted as the\u{A}beginning of a fenced code block.)\u{A}\u{A}The content of the code block consists of all subsequent lines, until\u{A}a closing [code fence] of the same type as the code block\u{A}began with (backticks or tildes), and with at least as many backticks\u{A}");
      out.append("or tildes as the opening code fence.  If the leading code fence is\u{A}preceded by N spaces of indentation, then up to N spaces of indentation are\u{A}removed from each line of the content (if present).  (If a content line is not\u{A}indented, it is preserved unchanged.  If it is indented N spaces or less, all\u{A}of the indentation is removed.)\u{A}\u{A}The closing code fence may be preceded by up to three spaces of indentation, and\u{A}may be followed only by spaces or tabs, which are ignored.  If the end of the\u{A}containing block (or document) is reached and no closing code fence\u{A}has been found, the code block contains all of the lines after the\u{A}opening code fence until the end of the containing block (or\u{A}document).  (An alternative spec would require backtracking in the\u{A}event that a closing code fence is not found.  But this makes parsing\u{A}much less efficient, and there seems to be no real downside to the\u{A}behavior described here.)\u{A}\u{A}A fenced code block may interrupt a paragraph, and does not require\u{A}a blank line either before or after.\u{A}\u{A}The content of a code fence is treated as literal text, not parsed\u{A}as inlines.  The first word of the [info string] is typically used to\u{A}specify the language of the code sample, and rendered in the `class`\u{A}attribute of the `code` tag.  However, this spec does not mandate any\u{A}particular treatment of the [info string].\u{A}\u{A}Here is a simple example with backticks:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}<\u{A} >\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>&lt;\u{A} &gt;\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}With tildes:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~\u{A}<\u{A} >\u{A}~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>&lt;\u{A} &gt;\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}Fewer than three backticks is not enough:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}``\u{A}foo\u{A}``\u{A}.\u{A}<p><code>foo</code></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}The closing code fence must use the same character as the opening\u{A}fence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}aaa\u{A}~~~\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}~~~\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}``````````````````````````");
      out.append("``````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~\u{A}aaa\u{A}```\u{A}~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}```\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The closing code fence must be at least as long as the opening fence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}````\u{A}aaa\u{A}```\u{A}``````\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}```\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~~\u{A}aaa\u{A}~~~\u{A}~~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}~~~\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Unclosed code blocks are closed by the end of the document\u{A}(or the enclosing [block quote][block quotes] or [list item][list items]):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code></code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}`````\u{A}\u{A}```\u{A}aaa\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>\u{A}```\u{A}aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}> ```\u{A}> aaa\u{A}\u{A}bbb\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}</blockquote>\u{A}<p>bbb</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A code block can have all empty lines as its content:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}  \u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>\u{A}  \u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A code block can be empty:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code></code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Fences can be indented.  If the opening fence is indented,\u{A}content lines will have equivalent opening indentation removed,\u{A}if present:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} ```\u{A} aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}  ```\u{A}aaa\u{A}  aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}  ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}   ```\u{A}   aaa\u{A}    aaa\u{A}  aaa\u{A}   ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A} aaa\u{A}aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Four spaces of indentation is too many:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}    ```\u{A}    aaa\u{A}    ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>```\u{A}aaa\u{A}```\u{A}<");
      out.append("/code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Closing fences may be preceded by up to three spaces of indentation, and their\u{A}indentation need not match that of the opening fence:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}aaa\u{A}  ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}   ```\u{A}aaa\u{A}  ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}This is not a closing fence, because it is indented 4 spaces:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}aaa\u{A}    ```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}    ```\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Code fences (opening and closing) cannot contain internal spaces or tabs:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}``` ```\u{A}aaa\u{A}.\u{A}<p><code> </code>\u{A}aaa</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~~~~\u{A}aaa\u{A}~~~ ~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>aaa\u{A}~~~ ~~\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Fenced code blocks can interrupt paragraphs, and can be followed\u{A}directly by paragraphs, without a blank line between:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}foo\u{A}```\u{A}bar\u{A}```\u{A}baz\u{A}.\u{A}<p>foo</p>\u{A}<pre><code>bar\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<p>baz</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Other blocks can also occur before and after fenced code blocks\u{A}without an intervening blank line:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}foo\u{A}---\u{A}~~~\u{A}bar\u{A}~~~\u{A}# baz\u{A}.\u{A}<h2>foo</h2>\u{A}<pre><code>bar\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<h1>baz</h1>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}An [info string] can be provided after the opening code fence.\u{A}Although this spec doesn't mandate any particular treatment of\u{A}the info string, the first word is typically used to specify\u{A}the language of the code block. In HTML output, the language is\u{A}normally indicated by adding a class to the `code` element consisting\u{A}of `language-` followed by the language name.\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```ruby\u{A}def foo(x)\u{A}  return 3\u{A}end\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-ruby\u{22}>def foo(x)\u{A}  return 3\u{A}end\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}`````");
      out.append("```````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~~    ruby startline=3 \u{24}%@#\u{24}\u{A}def foo(x)\u{A}  return 3\u{A}end\u{A}~~~~~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-ruby\u{22}>def foo(x)\u{A}  return 3\u{A}end\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}````;\u{A}````\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-;\u{22}></code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}[Info strings] for backtick code blocks cannot contain backticks:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}``` aa ```\u{A}foo\u{A}.\u{A}<p><code>aa</code>\u{A}foo</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}[Info strings] for tilde code blocks can contain backticks and tildes:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}~~~ aa ``` ~~~\u{A}foo\u{A}~~~\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code class=\u{22}language-aa\u{22}>foo\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Closing code fences cannot have [info strings]:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}```\u{A}``` aaa\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<pre><code>``` aaa\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}## HTML blocks\u{A}\u{A}An [HTML block](@) is a group of lines that is treated\u{A}as raw HTML (and will not be escaped in HTML output).\u{A}\u{A}There are seven kinds of [HTML block], which can be defined by their\u{A}start and end conditions.  The block begins with a line that meets a\u{A}[start condition](@) (after up to three optional spaces of indentation).\u{A}It ends with the first subsequent line that meets a matching\u{A}[end condition](@), or the last line of the document, or the last line of\u{A}the [container block](#container-blocks) containing the current HTML\u{A}block, if no line is encountered that meets the [end condition].  If\u{A}the first line meets both the [start condition] and the [end\u{A}condition], the block will contain just that line.\u{A}\u{A}1.  **Start condition:**  line begins with the string `<pre`,\u{A}`<script`, `<style`, or `<textarea` (case-insensitive), followed by a space,\u{A}a tab, the string `>`, or the end of the line.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:**  line contains an end tag\u{A}`</pre>`, `</script>`, `</style>`, or `</textarea>` (case-insensitive; it\u{A}need n");
      out.append("ot match the start tag).\u{A}\u{A}2.  **Start condition:** line begins with the string `<!--`.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:**  line contains the string `-->`.\u{A}\u{A}3.  **Start condition:** line begins with the string `<?`.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:** line contains the string `?>`.\u{A}\u{A}4.  **Start condition:** line begins with the string `<!`\u{A}followed by an ASCII letter.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:** line contains the character `>`.\u{A}\u{A}5.  **Start condition:**  line begins with the string\u{A}`<![CDATA[`.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:** line contains the string `]]>`.\u{A}\u{A}6.  **Start condition:** line begins with the string `<` or `</`\u{A}followed by one of the strings (case-insensitive) `address`,\u{A}`article`, `aside`, `base`, `basefont`, `blockquote`, `body`,\u{A}`caption`, `center`, `col`, `colgroup`, `dd`, `details`, `dialog`,\u{A}`dir`, `div`, `dl`, `dt`, `fieldset`, `figcaption`, `figure`,\u{A}`footer`, `form`, `frame`, `frameset`,\u{A}`h1`, `h2`, `h3`, `h4`, `h5`, `h6`, `head`, `header`, `hr`,\u{A}`html`, `iframe`, `legend`, `li`, `link`, `main`, `menu`, `menuitem`,\u{A}`nav`, `noframes`, `ol`, `optgroup`, `option`, `p`, `param`,\u{A}`search`, `section`, `summary`, `table`, `tbody`, `td`,\u{A}`tfoot`, `th`, `thead`, `title`, `tr`, `track`, `ul`, followed\u{A}by a space, a tab, the end of the line, the string `>`, or\u{A}the string `/>`.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:** line is followed by a [blank line].\u{A}\u{A}7.  **Start condition:**  line begins with a complete [open tag]\u{A}(with any [tag name] other than `pre`, `script`,\u{A}`style`, or `textarea`) or a complete [closing tag],\u{A}followed by zero or more spaces and tabs, followed by the end of the line.\u{5C}\u{A}**End condition:** line is followed by a [blank line].\u{A}\u{A}HTML blocks continue until they are closed by their appropriate\u{A}[end condition], or the last line of the document or other [container\u{A}block](#container-blocks).  This means any HTML **within an HTML\u{A}block** that might otherwise be recognised as a start condition will\u{A}be ignored by the parser and passed through as-is, without changing\u{A}the parser's state.\u{A}\u{A}For instance, `<pre>` within an ");
      out.append("HTML block started by `<table>` will not affect\u{A}the parser state; as the HTML block was started in by start condition 6, it\u{A}will end at any blank line. This can be surprising:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<table><tr><td>\u{A}<pre>\u{A}**Hello**,\u{A}\u{A}_world_.\u{A}</pre>\u{A}</td></tr></table>\u{A}.\u{A}<table><tr><td>\u{A}<pre>\u{A}**Hello**,\u{A}<p><em>world</em>.\u{A}</pre></p>\u{A}</td></tr></table>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}In this case, the HTML block is terminated by the blank line \u{2014} the `**Hello**`\u{A}text remains verbatim \u{2014} and regular parsing resumes, with a paragraph,\u{A}emphasised `world` and inline and block HTML following.\u{A}\u{A}All types of [HTML blocks] except type 7 may interrupt\u{A}a paragraph.  Blocks of type 7 may not interrupt a paragraph.\u{A}(This restriction is intended to prevent unwanted interpretation\u{A}of long tags inside a wrapped paragraph as starting HTML blocks.)\u{A}\u{A}Some simple examples follow.  Here are some basic HTML blocks\u{A}of type 6:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<table>\u{A}  <tr>\u{A}    <td>\u{A}           hi\u{A}    </td>\u{A}  </tr>\u{A}</table>\u{A}\u{A}okay.\u{A}.\u{A}<table>\u{A}  <tr>\u{A}    <td>\u{A}           hi\u{A}    </td>\u{A}  </tr>\u{A}</table>\u{A}<p>okay.</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A} <div>\u{A}  *hello*\u{A}         <foo><a>\u{A}.\u{A} <div>\u{A}  *hello*\u{A}         <foo><a>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A block can also start with a closing tag:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}</div>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}.\u{A}</div>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Here we have two HTML blocks with a Markdown paragraph between them:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<DIV CLASS=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}\u{A}*Markdown*\u{A}\u{A}</DIV>\u{A}.\u{A}<DIV CLASS=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}<p><em>Markdown</em></p>\u{A}</DIV>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The tag on the first line can be partial, as long\u{A}as it is split where there would be whitespace:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22}\u{A}  class=\u{22}bar\u{22}>\u{A}</div>\u{A}.\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22}\u{A}  class=\u{22}bar\u{22}>\u{A}</div>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22} c");
      out.append("lass=\u{22}bar\u{A}  baz\u{22}>\u{A}</div>\u{A}.\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22} class=\u{22}bar\u{A}  baz\u{22}>\u{A}</div>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}An open tag need not be closed:\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}\u{A}*bar*\u{A}.\u{A}<div>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}<p><em>bar</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A partial tag need not even be completed (garbage\u{A}in, garbage out):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22}\u{A}*hi*\u{A}.\u{A}<div id=\u{22}foo\u{22}\u{A}*hi*\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div class\u{A}foo\u{A}.\u{A}<div class\u{A}foo\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The initial tag doesn't even need to be a valid\u{A}tag, as long as it starts like one:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div *???-&&&-<---\u{A}*foo*\u{A}.\u{A}<div *???-&&&-<---\u{A}*foo*\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}In type 6 blocks, the initial tag need not be on a line by\u{A}itself:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div><a href=\u{22}bar\u{22}>*foo*</a></div>\u{A}.\u{A}<div><a href=\u{22}bar\u{22}>*foo*</a></div>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<table><tr><td>\u{A}foo\u{A}</td></tr></table>\u{A}.\u{A}<table><tr><td>\u{A}foo\u{A}</td></tr></table>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Everything until the next blank line or end of document\u{A}gets included in the HTML block.  So, in the following\u{A}example, what looks like a Markdown code block\u{A}is actually part of the HTML block, which continues until a blank\u{A}line or the end of the document is reached:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<div></div>\u{A}``` c\u{A}int x = 33;\u{A}```\u{A}.\u{A}<div></div>\u{A}``` c\u{A}int x = 33;\u{A}```\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}To start an [HTML block] with a tag that is *not* in the\u{A}list of block-level tags in (6), you must put the tag by\u{A}itself on the first line (and it must be complete):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<a href=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</a>\u{A}.\u{A}<a href=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</a>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}In type 7 blocks, the [tag name] can be anything:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<Warning>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</Warning>\u{A}.\u{A}<Warning>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</Warning");
      out.append(">\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<i class=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</i>\u{A}.\u{A}<i class=\u{22}foo\u{22}>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}</i>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}</ins>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}.\u{A}</ins>\u{A}*bar*\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}These rules are designed to allow us to work with tags that\u{A}can function as either block-level or inline-level tags.\u{A}The `<del>` tag is a nice example.  We can surround content with\u{A}`<del>` tags in three different ways.  In this case, we get a raw\u{A}HTML block, because the `<del>` tag is on a line by itself:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<del>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}</del>\u{A}.\u{A}<del>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}</del>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}In this case, we get a raw HTML block that just includes\u{A}the `<del>` tag (because it ends with the following blank\u{A}line).  So the contents get interpreted as CommonMark:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<del>\u{A}\u{A}*foo*\u{A}\u{A}</del>\u{A}.\u{A}<del>\u{A}<p><em>foo</em></p>\u{A}</del>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Finally, in this case, the `<del>` tags are interpreted\u{A}as [raw HTML] *inside* the CommonMark paragraph.  (Because\u{A}the tag is not on a line by itself, we get inline HTML\u{A}rather than an [HTML block].)\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<del>*foo*</del>\u{A}.\u{A}<p><del><em>foo</em></del></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}HTML tags designed to contain literal content\u{A}(`pre`, `script`, `style`, `textarea`), comments, processing instructions,\u{A}and declarations are treated somewhat differently.\u{A}Instead of ending at the first blank line, these blocks\u{A}end at the first line containing a corresponding end tag.\u{A}As a result, these blocks can contain blank lines:\u{A}\u{A}A pre tag (type 1):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<pre language=\u{22}haskell\u{22}><code>\u{A}import Text.HTML.TagSoup\u{A}\u{A}main :: IO ()\u{A}main = print \u{24} parseTags tags\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}okay\u{A}.\u{A}<pre language=\u{22}haskell\u{22}><code>\u{A}import Text.HTML.TagSoup\u{A}\u{A}main :: IO ()\u{A}main = print \u{24} parseTags tags\u{A}</code></pre>\u{A}<p>okay</p>\u{A}```````````````````````");
      out.append("`````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A script tag (type 1):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<script type=\u{22}text/javascript\u{22}>\u{A}// JavaScript example\u{A}\u{A}document.getElementById(\u{22}demo\u{22}).innerHTML = \u{22}Hello JavaScript!\u{22};\u{A}</script>\u{A}okay\u{A}.\u{A}<script type=\u{22}text/javascript\u{22}>\u{A}// JavaScript example\u{A}\u{A}document.getElementById(\u{22}demo\u{22}).innerHTML = \u{22}Hello JavaScript!\u{22};\u{A}</script>\u{A}<p>okay</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A textarea tag (type 1):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<textarea>\u{A}\u{A}*foo*\u{A}\u{A}_bar_\u{A}\u{A}</textarea>\u{A}.\u{A}<textarea>\u{A}\u{A}*foo*\u{A}\u{A}_bar_\u{A}\u{A}</textarea>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}A style tag (type 1):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<style\u{A}  type=\u{22}text/css\u{22}>\u{A}h1 {color:red;}\u{A}\u{A}p {color:blue;}\u{A}</style>\u{A}okay\u{A}.\u{A}<style\u{A}  type=\u{22}text/css\u{22}>\u{A}h1 {color:red;}\u{A}\u{A}p {color:blue;}\u{A}</style>\u{A}<p>okay</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}If there is no matching end tag, the block will end at the\u{A}end of the document (or the enclosing [block quote][block quotes]\u{A}or [list item][list items]):\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<style\u{A}  type=\u{22}text/css\u{22}>\u{A}\u{A}foo\u{A}.\u{A}<style\u{A}  type=\u{22}text/css\u{22}>\u{A}\u{A}foo\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}> <div>\u{A}> foo\u{A}\u{A}bar\u{A}.\u{A}<blockquote>\u{A}<div>\u{A}foo\u{A}</blockquote>\u{A}<p>bar</p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}- <div>\u{A}- foo\u{A}.\u{A}<ul>\u{A}<li>\u{A}<div>\u{A}</li>\u{A}<li>foo</li>\u{A}</ul>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}The end tag can occur on the same line as the start tag:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<style>p{color:red;}</style>\u{A}*foo*\u{A}.\u{A}<style>p{color:red;}</style>\u{A}<p><em>foo</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<!-- foo -->*bar*\u{A}*baz*\u{A}.\u{A}<!-- foo -->*bar*\u{A}<p><em>baz</em></p>\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}Note that anything on the last line after the\u{A}end tag will be included in the [HTML block]:\u{A}\u{A}```````````````````````````````` example\u{A}<script>\u{A}foo\u{A}</script>1. *bar*\u{A}.\u{A}<script>\u{A}foo\u{A}</script>1. *bar*\u{A}````````````````````````````````\u{A}\u{A}\u{A}A comment ");
