      out.append("2130706433`, `localhost.`, user info, backslashes and tabs, and IDNA\u{A}look-alikes), and on the site's recorded inputs.\u{A}\u{A}Each URL from `urltestdata.json` and `setters_tests.json` also checks\u{A}`strictHref`: it must be RFC 3986, by the grammar, and parse back to the\u{A}same parts once decoded. Their expected `href` is the standard's, so it's\u{A}checked against `hrefWithCredentials()`, and `href` and `strictHref` must\u{A}be the same without the credentials.\u{A}\u{A}## Site\u{A}\u{A}[The site](site/) shows the problem and the fix in one page: one URL that\u{A}other languages' parsers read as different hosts, a tester that parses\u{A}whatever you type live with Hrefine's JavaScript build (beside your\u{A}browser's own `URL`, with how browsers would show it, how `toString`\u{A}logs one with credentials, and each\u{A}validation error marked in the input), a gallery of tricks (short and hex IPv4 forms,\u{A}backslashes, `@` and `#@`, tabs, look-alike Unicode, `javascript:`, and\u{A}protocol-relative URLs), a table of 48 more disagreements, by kind\u{A}(backslashes and controls, user info, IPv4 and IPv6 forms, escaped and\u{A}international names, schemes and ports, and paths), and the fix in each\u{A}language, taken from the tours.\u{A}\u{A}Other parsers can't run in a browser, so their answers for the 62\u{A}recorded URLs are recorded ahead of time, with their versions and the date:\u{A}Python's `urllib.parse`, Java's `URI` and `URL`, .NET's `Uri`, Go's\u{A}`net/url`, libcurl's URL API, and Node's `URL`, standing in for browsers.\u{A}`site/record/record.py` runs each one that's installed on the inputs in\u{A}`site/gallery.json`, then on Hrefine's full forms of each,\u{A}`hrefWithCredentials()` and `strictHrefWithCredentials()` (the exact URL,\u{A}since some tricks are in the user info), and writes `site/recorded.json`, which is checked in. Each parser counts\u{A}as agreeing with browsers if it reads the same host (IPv6 addresses\u{A}compared as addresses), or for path tricks the same path. Given\u{A}`strictHref`, every recorded parser agrees on every URL Hrefine\u{A}accepts, but for Java's `");
      out.append("URL` and curl refusing `javascript:`; given\u{A}`href`, Java's `URI`, Go, and C# still miss a few paths (see the\u{A}[plan](docs/plan.md), step 6). The build fails unless browsers read\u{A}each strict form as they read its `href`. The table's inputs come\u{A}from web-platform-tests' cases and published research on parser\u{A}differences; keep it curated, with a short note on each. Re-record after changing the\u{A}gallery, or to update the versions:\u{A}\u{A}    temper build -b js -b py\u{A}    uv run site/record/record.py\u{A}\u{A}A scoreboard runs web-platform-tests' URL cases (`urltestdata.json`)\u{A}in the visitor's browser, through its own `URL` and through Hrefine,\u{A}lazily, once the section is near, and lists each miss. It counts as\u{A}Hrefine's own test does (each case must fail, or give the expected\u{A}`href` and every part), leaving out cases with unpaired surrogates, of\u{A}which this file has none. `site/scoreboard.js` holds the scoring, and the\u{A}build runs the same module in Node (`site/scoreboard-node.mjs`), showing\u{A}Node's score, with that Node's version, beside the visitor's, and failing\u{A}unless Hrefine passes every case. It warns if that Node isn't the one in\u{A}`recorded.json`.\u{A}\u{A}Build the site, with Temper, Python, and Node 24 or later, and preview\u{A}it:\u{A}\u{A}    temper build -b js\u{A}    uv run site/build.py\u{A}    python3 -m http.server --bind 127.0.0.1 -d site/build\u{A}\u{A}The build copies the JS library and Temper's runtime as plain ES modules\u{A}(no bundler, so about 80 KB gzipped), renders the opening, the gallery,\u{A}and the table from the recorded answers (each table row's full answers go\u{A}in `tables.json`, which the tester fetches only when one is tried), and takes the fix's code from each tour's\u{A}\u{22}Using it safely\u{22} section, failing if it can't find it. It also runs the\u{A}JavaScript tour in Node as the page's \u{22}Run in your browser\u{22} does\u{A}(`site/tourrun.js`, shared by both), failing unless it prints what every\u{A}tour prints.\u{A}\u{A}A \u{22}Show the code\u{22} switch puts badges on the parts of the page with code\u{A}behind them (`site/devmode.js`), each open");
      out.append("ing a short snippet in any of\u{A}the seven languages. The snippets are lines of the tours marked with a\u{A}comment such as `# snippet: hero, parts`; the build collects them into\u{A}`snippets.json`, failing if one is missing from a tour or a badge has\u{A}none, and strips the markers from the tours the page shows. It also\u{A}checks each name on a card, as each language spells it, against that\u{A}language's tour (`site/spell-node.mjs`). CI runs every tour, so every\u{A}snippet runs. Visits are counted\u{A}by cookieless GoatCounter, site code `hrefine`. CI\u{A}(`.github/workflows/site.yml`) builds the site on every change and\u{A}publishes it to GitHub Pages from `main`.\u{A}\u{A}## Languages\u{A}\u{A}Made in Temper, it will support these languages, and its tests run on\u{A}each:\u{A}\u{A}- C++\u{A}- C\u{5C}#\u{A}- Java\u{A}- JavaScript\u{A}- Lua\u{A}- Python\u{A}- Rust\u{A}\u{A}## Speed and size\u{A}\u{A}`bench/` times Hrefine on the same inputs in every language: 50 URLs\u{A}(`bench/urls.txt`: mostly ASCII, some against a base, IPv4 and IPv6\u{A}hosts, percent-encoding, nine international hosts, and three that must\u{A}fail) and 12 domains to convert to ASCII (`bench/domains.txt`). Each\u{A}program in `bench/<language>/` first checks its results against\u{A}`bench/expected.txt` (which match Node's `URL`), then reports the\u{A}library's load time, the first domain beyond ASCII, and steady-state\u{A}nanoseconds per URL and per domain; the script also measures the\u{A}generated library without tests, raw and gzipped. One command builds\u{A}and runs it all, five runs per language:\u{A}\u{A}    uv run bench/run.py [LANGUAGE ...]\u{A}\u{A}Medians on a busy laptop (2026-10-29), so rough:\u{A}\u{A}| Language | Load | First IDNA | Parse | IDNA | Size (gzipped) |\u{A}|---|---|---|---|---|---|\u{A}| C++ | 0.15 ms | 0.006 ms | 6.6 \u{B5}s | 2.7 \u{B5}s | 225 KB (43 KB) |\u{A}| C# | 4.4 ms | 11 ms (JIT) | 4.7 \u{B5}s | 4.6 \u{B5}s | 238 KB (41 KB) |\u{A}| Java | 4.1 ms | 0.45 ms | 7\u{2013}15 \u{B5}s | 1.6 \u{B5}s | 201 KB (40 KB) |\u{A}| JavaScript | 4.4 ms | 0.9 ms | 5.9 \u{B5}s | 5.9 \u{B5}s | 157 KB (40 KB) |\u{A}| Lua | 3.9 ms | 0.11 ms | 113 \u{B5}s | 132 \u{B5}s | 160 KB (40 KB) |\u{A}| Python | 1.6 ms | 0.13 ms | 106 \u{B5}s");
      out.append(" | 154 \u{B5}s | 163 KB (39 KB) |\u{A}| Rust | 0.08 ms | 0.009 ms | 7.2 \u{B5}s | 4.9 \u{B5}s | 248 KB (45 KB) |\u{A}\u{A}Load is after Temper's own runtime, which takes another 30\u{2013}40 ms to\u{A}import in Python. The API revision after the tours (`checkUrl`, typed\u{A}parts, and `parseHost`) left parsing as fast, timed in turns, and added\u{A}about 21\u{2013}23 KB (4 KB gzipped) in Python and JavaScript. The [plan](docs/plan.md) has what changed these.\u{A}The table predates the setters and search parameters (2026-10-30),\u{A}which add code, and a few checks to the parser for the setters' state\u{A}overrides; they aren't measured yet.\u{A}\u{A}## Roadmap\u{A}\u{A}See the [plan](docs/plan.md) for details.\u{A}\u{A}- **Done:** parsing and serializing, international domain names (IDNA),\u{A}  setters and search parameters, the strict RFC 3986 form, strict mode\u{A}  and policies, display formatting (with Unicode's script and security\u{A}  data), comparison, source spans, the\u{A}  tours, and the site.\u{A}- **Next:** lossless editing, designed in the\u{A}  plan, once its span and edit model is settled with a lossless\u{A}  Markdown and HTML library.\u{A}- **Before 1.0:** trademark checks, a minified site bundle, and\u{A}  publishing packages.\u{A}\u{A}## Development\u{A}\u{A}Written with Claude Code and Opus 5.5.\u{A}\u{A}Run tests with [Temper](https://temperlang.dev/), on one backend while\u{A}working:\u{A}\u{A}    temper test -b py\u{A}\u{A}Or on all of them:\u{A}\u{A}    temper test -b cpp -b csharp -b java -b js -b lua -b py -b rust\u{A}\u{A}Run the tour in every language against `temper.out/`, checking each\u{A}prints the same text (see [demos](demos/README.md)):\u{A}\u{A}    uv run demos/run.py\u{A}\u{A}CI runs the tests and the tour on every backend for pull requests and\u{A}pushes to `main`, using Temper's nightly `dev` build.\u{A}\u{A}Regenerate the tests from web-platform-tests' data, and the IDNA tables\u{A}from Unicode's (which `scripts/unicode_data.py` downloads and checks):\u{A}\u{A}    uv run scripts/wpt_tests.py\u{A}    uv run scripts/wpt_idna_tests.py\u{A}    uv run scripts/wpt_setter_tests.py\u{A}    uv run scripts/wpt_search_params_tests.py\u{A}    uv run scripts/idna_tables.py\u{A}    ");
      out.append("uv run scripts/display_tables.py\u{A}\u{A}## License\u{A}\u{A}Apache-2.0 OR MIT, at your option, as Temper is; see [COPYRIGHT](COPYRIGHT). Third-party material, with its notices, is in [NOTICE](NOTICE).\u{A}");
      out.toString()
    }

    let kelptoReadme(): String {
      let out = new StringBuilder();
      out.append("# Kelpto (working name)\u{A}\u{A}Lossless Markdown and a WHATWG HTML parser, written once in Temper, for\u{A}every language Temper supports. Parse a document, change one part, and\u{A}write it back with everything else exactly as it was. Render it safely,\u{A}with raw HTML parsed as browsers parse it and links checked by Hrefine.\u{A}\u{A}**Status** (2026-10-31): all of CommonMark parses losslessly and\u{A}renders as every one of the spec's 652 examples expects, and so do\u{A}GitHub Flavored Markdown's extensions (tables, task list items,\u{A}strikethrough, extended autolinks, and the disallowed raw HTML filter),\u{A}as GFM's examples expect; the HTML side is still a small skeleton.\u{A}The design is in [docs/design.md](docs/design.md) and the plan in\u{A}[docs/plan.md](docs/plan.md).\u{A}\u{A}| Library | What it is |\u{A}|---|---|\u{A}| [kelpto-core](kelpto-core/README.md) | The lossless red-green tree both parsers share. |\u{A}| [kelpto-md](kelpto-md/README.md) | Markdown: parse, edit, print exactly, and render. |\u{A}| [kelpto-html](kelpto-html/README.md) | HTML: tokenize, edit, and print exactly. |\u{A}| [kelpto](kelpto/README.md) | Both together: HTML inside Markdown, and safe rendering. |\u{A}\u{A}kelpto-md never imports kelpto-html. It finds raw HTML by CommonMark's\u{A}rules and asks a `RawHtmlHandler` what goes inside, and a\u{A}`RawHtmlRenderer` how to render it; `kelpto` fills both in with\u{A}kelpto-html.\u{A}\u{A}## Imagine using it\u{A}\u{A}These run today, on every backend.\u{A}\u{A}**Markdown alone,** in Temper:\u{A}\u{A}```temper\u{A}let doc = parseMarkdown(\u{22}# Old title\u{5C}n\u{5C}nSee <b>this</b>.\u{5C}n\u{5C}n```py\u{5C}nx = 1\u{5C}n```\u{5C}n\u{22});\u{A}doc.headings[0].text;                       // \u{22}Old title\u{22}\u{A}doc.headings[0].withText(\u{22}New title\u{22}).asWritten; // \u{22}# New title\u{5C}n\u{5C}nSee <b>this</b>.\u{5C}n\u{2026}\u{22}, the rest untouched\u{A}doc.toHtml();                               // \u{22}\u{2026}<p>See &lt;b&gt;this&lt;/b&gt;.</p>\u{2026}\u{22}, raw HTML escaped\u{A}new MarkdownRenderer().withRawHtmlTrusted().render(doc); // \u{22}\u{2026}<p>See <b>this</b>.</p>\u{2026}\u{22}\u{A}\u{A}let gfm = MarkdownParser.gfm.parse(\u{22}| a | b |\u{5C}n|---|--:|\u{5C}n| ~~x~~ | www.example.com |\u{5C}n\u{5C}n- [ ] todo\u{5C}n\u{22});\u{A}gfm.table");
      out.append("s[0].rows[0].cells[1].alignment;   // \u{22}right\u{22}\u{A}gfm.taskListItems[0].withChecked(true).asWritten; // \u{22}\u{2026}- [x] todo\u{5C}n\u{22}\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}**HTML alone:**\u{A}\u{A}```temper\u{A}let a = tokenizeHtml(\u{22}<a href=\u{5C}\u{22}/old\u{5C}\u{22} class=x>Go</a>\u{22}).startTags[0];\u{A}a.name;                                     // \u{22}a\u{22}\u{A}(a.attribute(\u{22}href\u{22}) as Attribute orelse panic()).withValue(\u{22}/new\u{22}).asWritten;\u{A}                                            // \u{22}<a href=\u{5C}\u{22}/new\u{5C}\u{22} class=x>Go</a>\u{22}\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}**Together:**\u{A}\u{A}```temper\u{A}let doc = parseMarkdownWithHtml(\u{22}Hi <b class=x>there</b><script>alert(1)</script>\u{22});\u{A}htmlOf(doc.rawHtml[0]).startTags[0].name;   // \u{22}b\u{22}: an HTML subtree in the Markdown tree\u{A}renderSafe(doc).html;                       // \u{22}<p>Hi <b>there</b>alert(1)</p>\u{5C}n\u{22}\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}The same in **Python**:\u{A}\u{A}```python\u{A}from kelpto_md import parse_markdown, MarkdownRenderer\u{A}from kelpto_html import tokenize_html\u{A}from kelpto import parse_markdown_with_html, html_of, render_safe\u{A}\u{A}doc = parse_markdown(\u{22}# Old title\u{5C}n\u{5C}nSee <b>this</b>.\u{5C}n\u{5C}n```py\u{5C}nx = 1\u{5C}n```\u{5C}n\u{22})\u{A}doc.headings[0].with_text(\u{22}New title\u{22}).as_written\u{A}doc.code_blocks[0].with_info(\u{22}python\u{22}).as_written  # \u{22}\u{2026}```python\u{5C}n\u{2026}\u{22}\u{A}doc.to_html()\u{A}\u{A}from kelpto_md import MarkdownParser\u{A}gfm = MarkdownParser.gfm.parse(\u{22}| a | b |\u{5C}n|---|--:|\u{5C}n| x | www.example.com |\u{5C}n\u{22})\u{A}gfm.tables[0].rows[0].cells[0].with_text(\u{22}y\u{22}).as_written\u{A}\u{A}a = tokenize_html('<a href=\u{22}/old\u{22} class=x>Go</a>').start_tags[0]\u{A}[(x.name, x.value) for x in a.attributes]          # [(\u{22}href\u{22}, \u{22}/old\u{22}), (\u{22}class\u{22}, \u{22}x\u{22})]\u{A}a.attribute(\u{22}href\u{22}).with_value(\u{22}/new\u{22}).as_written\u{A}\u{A}both = parse_markdown_with_html(\u{22}Hi <b class=x>there</b><script>alert(1)</script>\u{22})\u{A}render_safe(both).html                             # \u{22}<p>Hi <b>there</b>alert(1)</p>\u{5C}n\u{22}\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}And **JavaScript**:\u{A}\u{A}```js\u{A}import { parseMarkdown, MarkdownRenderer } from \u{22}kelpto-md\u{22};\u{A}import { tokenizeHtml } from \u{22}kelpto-html\u{22};\u{A}import { parseMarkdownWithHtml, htmlOf, renderSafe } from \u{22}kelpto\u{22};\u{A}\u{A}const doc = parseMarkdown(\u{22}# Old title\u{5C}n\u{5C}nSee <b>this</b>.\u{5C}n\u{22});\u{A}doc.headings[0].withText(\u{22}New title\u{22}).asWritten;\u{A}n");
      out.append("ew MarkdownRenderer().withRawHtmlTrusted().render(doc);\u{A}\u{A}const a = tokenizeHtml('<a href=\u{22}/old\u{22} class=x>Go</a>').startTags[0];\u{A}a.attribute(\u{22}href\u{22}).withValue(\u{22}/new\u{22}).asWritten;  // '<a href=\u{22}/new\u{22} class=x>Go</a>'\u{A}\u{A}const both = parseMarkdownWithHtml(\u{22}Hi <b class=x>there</b><script>alert(1)</script>\u{22});\u{A}htmlOf(both.rawHtml[0]).startTags[0].name;        // \u{22}b\u{22}\u{A}renderSafe(both).html;\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}`renderSafe` is a sketch, not a sanitizer yet; see\u{A}[kelpto/README.md](kelpto/README.md).\u{A}\u{A}## Tests and licensing\u{A}\u{A}Each library has its own tests. kelpto-md's tests against CommonMark's\u{A}spec examples are in [tests/commonmark](tests/commonmark/README.md),\u{A}and against GFM's, from cmark-gfm, in [tests/gfm](tests/gfm/README.md):\u{A}test-only libraries, since the examples are CC-BY-SA 4.0; see\u{A}[NOTICE](NOTICE). `scripts/commonmark_spec.py` and\u{A}`scripts/gfm_spec.py` (run with `uv run`) regenerate them.\u{A}\u{A}## Building\u{A}\u{A}One Temper worktree with a library in each directory; run `temper test`\u{A}at the top. Libraries import each other by path, as\u{A}`import(\u{22}../kelpto-core\u{22})`.\u{A}\u{A}Run the tour in every language against `temper.out/`, checking each\u{A}prints the same text (see [demos](demos/README.md)):\u{A}\u{A}    temper build -b cpp -b csharp -b java -b js -b lua -b py -b rust\u{A}    uv run demos/run.py [LANGUAGE ...]\u{A}\u{A}Build the site, with the JavaScript build, and serve it locally (see\u{A}[docs/site.md](docs/site.md)):\u{A}\u{A}    temper build -b js\u{A}    uv run site/build.py\u{A}    python3 -m http.server --bind 127.0.0.1 -d site/build\u{A}\u{A}## License\u{A}\u{A}Apache-2.0 OR MIT, at your option, as Temper is; see [COPYRIGHT](COPYRIGHT). Third-party material, with its notices, is in [NOTICE](NOTICE). The CommonMark and GFM examples in [tests/commonmark](tests/commonmark/README.md) and [tests/gfm](tests/gfm/README.md) stay under CC-BY-SA 4.0, apart from the libraries' code, and aren't published.\u{A}");
      out.toString()
    }

    let ordableReadme(): String {
      let out = new StringBuilder();
      out.append("# Ordable\u{A}\u{A}Sorts strings in smart order, the way people expect: `file2` before\u{A}`file10`, since runs of digits compare as numbers. It's often called\u{A}natural or [human sorting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_sort_order); Ordable says \u{22}smart\u{22}, since in Java and\u{A}Kotlin \u{22}natural order\u{22} (`naturalOrder()`) means a type's own order, which\u{A}for strings is the plain code-unit order Ordable replaces.\u{A}\u{A}## Examples\u{A}\u{A}In Temper, and the same API in each supported language:\u{A}\u{A}```temper\u{A}let names = [\u{22}img12.png\u{22}, \u{22}img10.png\u{22}, \u{22}img2.png\u{22}, \u{22}img1.png\u{22}];\u{A}sortSmart(names); // img1.png img2.png img10.png img12.png\u{A}\u{A}compareSmart(\u{22}file2\u{22}, \u{22}file10\u{22}); // Negative: file2 comes first\u{A}compareSmart(\u{22}a\u{22}, \u{22}B\u{22}); // Negative: case only breaks ties\u{A}\u{A}// Any items, by a string from each, such as files by name.\u{A}sortSmartByKey(files) { file => file.name };\u{A}\u{A}// A string that sorts in smart order by plain comparison, as in a database.\u{A}smartKey(\u{22}file10\u{22}); // Sorts after smartKey(\u{22}file2\u{22})\u{A}\u{A}// Options: an Order has the same functions as methods.\u{A}let caseFirst: Order = { casing: Casing.first, upperFirst: true };\u{A}caseFirst.sort(names); // Every IMG before every img, as plain sorting does\u{A}caseFirst.sortByKey(files) { file => file.name };\u{A}```\u{A}\u{A}Also: a [tour](demos/README.md) of the whole API in each language, with\u{A}identical output, and a [site](site/) to try smart sorting in the\u{A}browser, beside plain sorting and `Intl.Collator`.\u{A}\u{A}## Rules\u{A}\u{A}- Runs of decimal digits compare by value, at any length, without\u{A}  overflow. Digits in any script count (Unicode 15.0), such as Arabic-Indic\u{A}  `\u{662}` or fullwidth `\u{FF12}`, and a run is all from one script.\u{A}- Letters compare without their accents or case first, so `\u{E9}` and `\u{C9}`\u{A}  sort with `e`, and letters fold fully, so `\u{3A3}` matches `\u{3C2}` and `\u{DF}`\u{A}  matches `ss`. An accent written as a combining mark after its letter\u{A}  (`e` and U+0301) counts as the precomposed letter would (`\u{E9}`), just\u{A}  after it. Everything else compares by code point.\u{A}- Two strings compare equal only if they're the sa");
      out.append("me, so sorting gives one\u{A}  answer whatever the input order. Leading zeros (`1` before `01`), the\u{A}  digits' script (`1` before `\u{661}`), accents (`e` before `\u{E9}`), case\u{A}  (`a` before `A`), and odd forms (`K` before the Kelvin sign) only break\u{A}  ties, in that order.\u{A}- Options, in an `Order`: when case is considered, its `casing`, from\u{A}  `Casing.ignored` (`File` equals `file`), through `Casing.last` (the\u{A}  default: case only breaks ties), to `Casing.first` (case before the\u{A}  letter, so every `img` comes before every `IMG`); `upperFirst`,\u{A}  for uppercase first wherever case counts; `ignoreAccents` (`\u{E9}`\u{A}  equals `e`); and `ignoreNumberForms` (`01`, `1`, and `\u{661}` are equal,\u{A}  as with `Intl.Collator`'s numeric option). With these, different\u{A}  strings can compare equal, with the same keys.\u{A}- No locale rules: that's for ICU or the platform.\u{A}\u{A}Ordable's Unicode tables (decimal digits, case folding as 202 compact\u{A}runs plus 104 full foldings, 977 accented letters' base letters, 30 odd\u{A}forms, combining marks as 346 runs, and 905 compositions of a letter and\u{A}a mark) are\u{A}generated by `uv run scripts/unicode_tables.py`, which checks them\u{A}against Python's Unicode data for every code point, pinned to Unicode\u{A}15.0.0. They're internal, so Ordable can switch to Unicode support built\u{A}into Temper later without changing its API. Smart keys depend on them,\u{A}so a release that changes keys (a new Unicode version, say) will say so.\u{A}\u{A}## Storing keys\u{A}\u{A}A smart key (`smartKey`, or `key` on an `Order`) sorts by plain\u{A}comparison exactly as its string sorts in smart order, so it can be\u{A}stored and sorted anywhere, such as a database column with `ORDER BY`:\u{A}\u{A}- **Compare keys as plain code points.** In a database, give the column\u{A}  a binary collation: `COLLATE \u{22}C\u{22}` in PostgreSQL, a binary collation\u{A}  such as `utf8mb4_bin` in MySQL, or SQLite's default. A\u{A}  language-aware or case-insensitive collation would reorder keys.\u{A}- **Keys contain control characters,** such as U+0001 between levels, so\u{A}  they're not for X");
      out.append("ML 1.0, which forbids them, nor for showing to\u{A}  people. They stay below U+D800 and never contain U+0000, so UTF-8,\u{A}  UTF-16, and code points all order them alike.\u{A}- **Keys say what made them.** Each starts with `0`, for this key\u{A}  format (later formats count up from it), and then a code point for\u{A}  the options that made it, so keys\u{A}  from other options or another format are easy to spot. Compare only\u{A}  keys made the same way.\u{A}- **Keys don't change by accident:** every backend's tests check a\u{A}  frozen set of exact keys ([key vectors](src/key-vectors.temper.md)).\u{A}  A change to keys would come with a new format code point and a new\u{A}  major version.\u{A}\u{A}## Roadmap\u{A}\u{A}- **Done:** smart order with numbers of any length, a total order,\u{A}  sorting by key, decimal digits in any script, and Unicode case folding.\u{A}- **Compared** with the usual tools: see\u{A}  [docs/comparisons.md](docs/comparisons.md).\u{A}- **Done:** smart sort keys (`smartKey`), which `sortSmart` uses\u{A}  for lists past a few items, and which Python can sort by and databases\u{A}  can `ORDER BY`.\u{A}- **Done:** accents sort with their base letter (`e`, `\u{E9}`, `f`), and `\u{DF}`\u{A}  matches `ss`.\u{A}- **Done:** defaults as people expect, with case only breaking ties\u{A}  (lowercase first), and an `Order` class for options (`casing` and\u{A}  `upperFirst`).\u{A}- **Done:** options that let different strings compare equal\u{A}  (`ignoreAccents`, `Casing.ignored`, and `ignoreNumberForms`, which\u{A}  makes `01`, `1`, and `\u{661}` equal, as `Intl.Collator`'s numeric option\u{A}  does), with matching keys.\u{A}- **Done:** `Order.sortByKey`, sorting any items by a string from each,\u{A}  with an order's options.\u{A}- **Done:** decomposed text: `e` plus a combining accent sorts as `\u{E9}`,\u{A}  just after it.\u{A}- **Next:** smarter punctuation, checked against real-world samples; and more\u{A}  options: signed and decimal numbers.\u{A}- **A spec:** the source as a readable specification of the default\u{A}  order and every option, with its tests beside it, and comparison\u{A}  tables so people can choose well.\u{A}- ");
      out.append("**Started:** a [showcase site](site/), where you pick or type lines\u{A}  and see smart order beside plain sorting and `Intl.Collator`, with\u{A}  the code in every language.\u{A}- **Done:** compact smart keys, usually about as long as their string:\u{A}  levels after the first only record what's unusual (accents, uppercase,\u{A}  leading zeros), so `file10`'s key is 8 code points.\u{A}- **Quick tests, long runs on demand:** the property tests compare every\u{A}  pair of generated strings, so `generatedCount` in\u{A}  [src/properties.temper.md](src/properties.temper.md) keeps them quick;\u{A}  raise it for a long run or a benchmark. Later, property testing and\u{A}  benchmarks across backends could be built into Temper itself.\u{A}- **Done:** a key format to keep: a header naming the format and the\u{A}  options, frozen key vectors checked on every backend, and notes on\u{A}  storing keys.\u{A}- **Done:** named it \u{22}smart\u{22} (`sortSmart`, `compareSmart`, `smartKey`):\u{A}  what's usually called natural sorting, since in Java and Kotlin\u{A}  \u{22}natural order\u{22} means a type's own `compareTo`, the plain order Ordable\u{A}  replaces.\u{A}\u{A}## Languages\u{A}\u{A}Made in Temper, Ordable supports these languages, and its tests run on each:\u{A}\u{A}- C++\u{A}- C\u{5C}#\u{A}- Java\u{A}- JavaScript\u{A}- Lua\u{A}- Python\u{A}- Rust\u{A}\u{A}## Development\u{A}\u{A}Written with Claude Code and Opus 5.5.\u{A}\u{A}Run tests with [Temper](https://temperlang.dev/), on one backend while\u{A}working:\u{A}\u{A}    temper test -b py\u{A}\u{A}Or on all of them:\u{A}\u{A}    temper test -b cpp -b csharp -b java -b js -b lua -b py -b rust\u{A}\u{A}Run the tour in every language against `temper.out/`, checking each prints\u{A}the same text (see [demos](demos/README.md)):\u{A}\u{A}    uv run demos/run.py\u{A}\u{A}Build and preview the site, which uses the JS backend's output:\u{A}\u{A}    temper build -b js\u{A}    npm ci --prefix site\u{A}    uv run site/build.py\u{A}    python3 -m http.server --bind 127.0.0.1 -d site/build\u{A}\u{A}CI runs the tests and the tour on every backend for pull requests and\u{A}pushes to `main`, using Temper's nightly `dev` build, and publishes the\u{A}site from `main`. Source files in `src/`\u{A}a");
      out.append("re literate Markdown, with tests next to the code they cover.\u{A}\u{A}## License\u{A}\u{A}Apache-2.0 OR MIT, at your option, as Temper is; see [COPYRIGHT](COPYRIGHT). Third-party material, with its notices, is in [NOTICE](NOTICE).\u{A}");
      out.toString()
    }
